     1	# Verifying Murage
     2	
     3	Murage has one development control surface: `pnpm control:murage`. It is a
     4	thin command-line adapter over `scripts/mcp-server.ts`, so verification uses
     5	the same URL validation, task pinning, bounded transcripts, wait states, and
     6	redaction as external MCP clients.
     7	
     8	## Launch
     9	
    10	Start a fixture in one terminal:
    11	
    12	```sh
    13	node --experimental-strip-types scripts/control-murage.ts launch
    14	```
    15	
    16	Run the foreground launcher directly rather than through `pnpm`; this ensures
    17	it receives Ctrl-C and can stop its child before removing the temporary data.
    18	
    19	It gives the child a temporary data directory and home, chooses a free
    20	harness/webhook port pair, installs only the repository's fake engine, prints
    21	the URL, PID, data directory, and persistent log path, then stays attached to
    22	that exact child. The parent shell and the user's Murage data are
    23	untouched.
    24	
    25	Pass the printed URL explicitly from a second terminal:
    26	
    27	```sh
    28	pnpm control:murage doctor --url http://127.0.0.1:PORT
    29	```
    30	
    31	Mutating commands refuse silent port discovery. This prevents a verification
    32	recipe from sending messages to the user's running app by accident.
    33	
    34	## Drive
    35	
    36	Use only mapped, tested commands:
    37	
    38	- [Chat turns](chat-turns.md)
    39	- [Channels](channels.md)
    40	- [Engines and Doctor](engines.md)
    41	
    42	Renderer-only behavior—Settings, sidebar drag-and-drop, the VM modal, the
    43	built-in browser panel, and updater UI—is not proven by this first harness.
    44	Use the relevant Electron/package smoke test and state that limitation. Add a
    45	map entry only after the shared control surface can really drive it.
    46	
    47	## Evidence
    48	
    49	Keep the JSON from `wait` and `messages`, the exact command sequence, and the
    50	fixture's printed log path. Evidence must show both the action and the resulting
    51	state. A green unit test alone does not prove a user workflow.
    52	
    53	## Cleanup
    54	
    55	Interrupt the `launch` process with Ctrl-C. It stops the exact child it owns and
    56	removes only its temporary data directory. The server log remains at the
    57	printed path. Never kill processes by name and never delete a broad temp root.
   560	4. **Gate + backstop + 409 + capability flag** (§5) — *before* any row ships.
   561	5. **claude surface** (§4.1) — the best-verified of the three; also patch
   562	   claude.ts:669 and claude.ts:123.
   563	6. **qwen surface** (§4.3) — after the `-m` vs `OPENAI_BASE_URL` question is
   564	   settled by a live turn.
   565	7. **codex surface** (§4.2) — **last**, and only after the `shell` →
   566	   `local_shell` rename is exercised end-to-end in a real codex turn. That
   567	   rename may block codex entirely.
   568	8. **avatar-image.ts** — do not attempt (§7).
   569	
   570	---
   571	
   572	## 9. UNVERIFIED — do not build against these
   573	
   574	**Blocked by the 402 wall (probe account is currently billing-blocked):**
   575	- `text.format` / `json_schema` structured outputs on `/v1/responses`. The one
   576	  attempt before the 402 returned a bare `error code: 502` Cloudflare
   577	  plain-text body — so error bodies are **not always JSON**.
   578	- `max_output_tokens` truncation and `incomplete_details` behaviour.
   579	
   580	**Never probed on the chat-completions surface:**
   581	- Streaming (`stream: true`) — confirmed on the Anthropic and Responses
   582	  surfaces, **not** on `/v1/chat/completions`.
   583	- Tool calling on `/v1/chat/completions`.
   584	- 4xx error body shapes on `/v1/chat/completions`.
   585	- Whether `flux-auto` routing changes with prompt complexity.
   586	  `x-flux-tier-escalated: false` and `x-flux-summarization-applied: false` on
   587	  both calls imply escalation/summarization paths exist but were not exercised.
   588	
   589	**Never probed at all:**
   590	- The entire **image success path** (§7) — `b64_json` vs `url`, MIME, whether
   591	  `quality`/`output_format`/`size` are honoured.
   592	- `flux-voice` (present in `/v1/models`, untouched).
   593	- Whether qwen resolves `-m <model>` against `OPENAI_BASE_URL` or against
   594	  `~/.qwen` modelProviders (§4.3) — decides whether the Flux model id must be
   595	  settled at core.ts:316 or core.ts:317.
   596	- Whether Claude Code accepts `flux-auto` as `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` without
   597	  client-side model validation.
   598	- Whether the `shell` → `local_shell` rename actually breaks a codex turn, or
   599	  whether codex tolerates it (§4.2).
   600	- Semantic-cache hit rate and whether a cached answer is ever distinguishable.
   601	  Nothing in the body flags a cache hit; only `cached_tokens` /
   602	  `cache_read_input_tokens` hint at it.
   603	- Rate limits and concurrency behaviour on any surface.
   604	- Whether the 402 is specific to the probe key or account-wide.
   605	
   606	**Not evidence-backed in this repo:**
   607	- `OPENAI_MODEL` — zero references anywhere in `server/`, `scripts/`,
   608	  `drivers/`. Any inclusion in `ROUTING_ENV` or in the qwen injection is
   609	  defensive only.

   613	# Probe round 2 — 2026-09-01, after the key was reported fixed
   614	
   615	Live re-probe against `api.fluxrouter.ai` with the same key. The account
   619	## R2.1 The 402 is RECURRING, not resolved
   620	
   621	Observed twice now, same shape both times:
   622	
   623	| Round | Calls served before re-block |
   624	|---|---|
   625	| Workflow probe | ~13 calls, ≈ $0.003 |
   626	| Round 2 | ~15 calls |
   627	
   628	After re-blocking: `chat=402 models=200` on three consecutive tries 10s apart.
   629	The message is identical each time — *"A prior charge on this account could not
   630	be reconciled automatically and requires support intervention."*
   631	
   632	**This is a Flux Router billing-reconciliation defect, not a Murage concern.**
   633	But it dictates one Murage design rule: **any Flux turn can 402 mid-session
   634	without warning.** The 402 path must surface a real message and fall back to
   635	native routing, never retry-loop.
   636	
   637	`GET /v1/models` returned **200 throughout both block windows.** §6.4 stands:
   638	never use the models list as a liveness probe.
   639	
   640	## R2.2 Newly CONFIRMED WORKING (clears four §9 items)
   641	
   642	| Capability | Verdict |
   643	|---|---|
   644	| `/v1/chat/completions` streaming (`stream:true`) | **WORKS** — SSE, standard `chat.completion.chunk` |
   645	| `/v1/chat/completions` tool calling | **WORKS** — standard `tool_calls[]`, `finish_reason:"tool_calls"` |
   646	| `max_output_tokens` truncation | **WORKS** — `status:"incomplete"`, `incomplete_details:{reason:"max_output_tokens"}` |
   647	| `/v1/responses` function calling | **WORKS** |
   648	
   649	**Streaming gotcha:** the first deltas carry `reasoning_content`, not
   650	`content` — `{"delta":{"reasoning_content":"We","role":"assistant"}}`. A client
   651	that reads only `delta.content` sees empty frames and may render nothing or
   652	close the stream early. Handle or discard `reasoning_content` explicitly.
   653	
   654	## R2.3 `shell` → `local_shell` — CONFIRMED, and it is a CONTRACT SUBSTITUTION
   655	
   656	Reproduced 5/5 across two sessions. Trigger is the **exact** tool name `shell`.
   657	
   658	```
   659	sent=shell          returned=local_shell    args={"command": ["ls", "-la"]}
   660	sent=local_shell    returned=local_shell
   661	sent=run_shell      returned=run_shell        <- passes through
   662	sent=shell_exec     returned=shell_exec       <- passes through
   663	sent=bash           returned=bash             <- passes through
   664	sent=myshell        returned=myshell          <- passes through
   665	sent=get_weather_v2 returned=get_weather_v2   <- passes through
   666	```
   667	
   668	Not merely a rename. The declared schema was `command: {type: "string"}`; the
   669	returned call carries `command: ["ls","-la"]` — an **array**. The gateway
   670	substitutes OpenAI's native `local_shell` tool contract over the caller's
   671	declared function. A client that declares `shell` gets back a call it did not
   672	declare, carrying arguments of a type it did not ask for.
   673	
   674	**Codex CLI declares `shell`.** Build step 7 stays last and stays gated on a
   675	real end-to-end codex turn.
   676	
   677	**CORRECTION to round 1:** the `run_shell` / `shell_exec` 502s recorded earlier
   678	were **transient, not name-triggered** — both return 200 and pass through on
   679	re-probe. The responses surface throws sporadic 502s independent of payload.
   680	
   681	## R2.4 Structured outputs — silently ignored (INCONCLUSIVE, re-probe needed)
   682	
   683	`text.format.json_schema` with `strict:true` on `flux-auto` returned **prose,
   684	not JSON**, with `status:"completed"` and **no error**. The request routed to
   685	`us.amazon.nova-pro-v1:0`.
   686	
   687	Unresolved: whether Flux drops the param, or routes to a model that cannot
   688	honour it and LiteLLM strips it silently. Re-probes on
   689	`flux-pinned-claude-opus-5` and `flux-reasoning` both hit the 402 before
   690	answering.
   691	
   692	**Either way this is the dangerous failure mode** — a silent contract break
   693	that only shows up at `JSON.parse`. **Do not rely on structured output from
   694	Flux until this is settled.**
   695	
   696	## R2.5 Error shapes — one round-1 finding REVERSED, two real bugs
   697	
   698	**REVERSED — unknown model is now cleanly distinguishable.** Round 1 recorded
   699	unknown model → 401, indistinguishable from a bad key. On `/v1/chat/completions`
   700	it now returns **403** with a genuinely well-written message:
   701	
   702	```json
   703	{"error":{"message":"This key is not permitted to use flux-nope-9000.
   704	 The key itself is valid — regenerating it will not change this.
   705	 Contact support@fluxrouter.ai if it should have access.",
   706	 "type":"auth_error","param":"model","code":"403"}}
   707	```
   708	
   709	Two real defects remain, both **5xx where a 4xx belongs**:
   710	
   711	| Request | Returns | Should be |
   712	|---|---|---|
   713	| No `Authorization` header | **500** `"Flux Router error: No api key passed in."` | 401 |
   714	| Missing `messages` | **500** `"Router.acompletion() missing 1 required positional argument: 'messages'"` | 400 |
   715	
   716	The second leaks a Python framework traceback to the caller. The first is
   717	worse operationally: **clients retry 5xx and do not retry 401**, so a
   718	missing or malformed key produces a retry storm instead of a clean failure.
   719	Murage must not treat a Flux 500 as retryable without inspecting the body.
   720	
   721	## R2.6 CORRECTION to §6.1 — `model` echo differs BY SURFACE
   722	
   723	Round 1 concluded "never trust the body's `model`". True only on one surface:
   724	
   725	| Surface | `model` in body |
   726	|---|---|
   727	| `/v1/chat/completions` | echoes the **alias** (`flux-auto` → `"flux-auto"`) |
   728	| `/v1/responses` | returns the **resolved upstream** (`flux-fast` → `"mistral-small-latest"`) |
   729	| `/anthropic/v1/messages` | returns the **resolved upstream** (`flux-fast` → `"mistral-small-latest"`) |
   730	
   731	This matters for claude (§4.1): Claude Code sees a `model` in the response that
   732	is not the one it sent. Confirm it does not validate the echo before shipping
   733	that surface.
   734	
   735	Also re-confirmed: `flux-auto` resolutions now include `qwen-plus`,
   736	`us.amazon.nova-pro-v1:0` and `mistral-small-latest` on top of round 1's list.
   737	
   738	## R2.7 Images route — STILL DEAD, and now provably not a billing symptom
   739	
   740	`POST /v1/images/generations` returned **502** while the account was serving
   741	200s on all three inference surfaces. Every other route hit the billing gate
   742	first and returned 402; this one returned 502 in both states.
   743	
   744	Two conclusions: the image backend is genuinely down, **and the image route
   745	appears to bypass the billing layer entirely** — worth checking on the Flux
   746	side, since image generations may not be billed once it serves.
   747	
   748	**§7 stands unchanged: leave `avatar-image.ts` on OpenAI.**
     1	# Flux Router in Murage — the key, the moment, the copy
     2	
     3	**Status:** plan for cross-audit. 2026-09-01
     4	**Reality check:** ZERO Flux code exists in Murage today. `grep -i flux server src electron`
     5	returns nothing. The surfaces are verified live (`flux-router-spec.md`); none of it is wired.
     6	
     7	## Situation
     8	
     9	Murage finds the agent CLIs already on your machine and runs them with your existing
    10	logins. That works, and it is also the ceiling: the user pays five vendors, holds five
    11	subscriptions, and still has to decide which model to point at which job.
    12	
    13	Flux Router is Ferrox's own OpenAI-compatible router. One key, any supported model,
    14	automatic tier selection. It is first-party, so it is also margin — which means it has to
    15	EARN the pick, never be switched on for people.
    16	
    17	Verified live against the real key: `/v1/chat/completions`, `/anthropic/v1/messages`,
    18	`/v1/responses` and `/v1/models` all 200. 79 models. `/v1/images/generations` is down.
    19	
    20	## The problem, stated honestly
    21	
    22	The user's problem is NOT "I need an inference router." Nobody wakes up wanting one.
    23	
    24	The real problem is **decision fatigue and quiet waste**. They have Claude, ChatGPT,
    25	maybe Gemini. They do not know whether the expensive model was needed for the thing they
    26	just asked. They suspect they are overpaying and they cannot prove it. Every model picker
    27	in every app is a small tax on attention.
    28	
    29	## Rory Sutherland: sell the reframe, not the plumbing
    30	
    31	Sutherland's point is that the psychological problem is usually the real problem. Uber's
    32	breakthrough was not faster cars — it was the map showing the car approaching, which
    33	removed uncertainty. Same cars, transformed experience.
    34	
    35	Applied here: **we are not selling cheaper inference. We are selling the end of choosing.**
    36	
    37	- "Router" is plumbing language. It describes our side of the transaction.
    38	- "One key. Every model. Always the right one." describes theirs.
    39	- The tiers are not products, they are *intentions*: Auto (just handle it), Reasoning
    40	  (think hard), Fast (I'm in a hurry), Standard (the everyday one).
    41	
    42	A user who never opens the picker again has received the whole benefit. That is the goal
    43	state, and it should be stated as the promise rather than buried as a side effect.
    44	
    45	## Steve Krug: don't make me think
    46	
    47	Krug's rules, applied literally:
    48	
    49	1. **One field, one paste, no vocabulary.** Never say router, endpoint, base URL, surface,
    50	   provider or tier on the entry screen. The user pastes a key. That is the whole task.
    51	2. **Never explain what it is before showing what it does.** Proof first.
    52	3. **Every question the screen provokes is a defect.** "Which models?" "Does this replace
    53	   my Claude sub?" "What does it cost?" — answer those inline, in one line each, or the
    54	   user stalls.
    55	4. **Skip must be one click and unpunished.** No "are you sure", no dimmed guilt copy.
    56	   The offer has to earn the yes; a coerced yes is worth less than a clean no.
    57	
    58	## Where it goes — three surfaces
    59	
    60	### 1. Onboarding, immediately AFTER the engine list
    61	
    62	Step 1 already says "Your engines — here's what we found" and lists what is installed.
    63	That screen is the exact moment the user is thinking about what powers their bots, and it
    64	has just proved we found their stuff. The offer stands on that proof.
    65	
    66	Ordering is load-bearing: the list first, then the offer. Reversed, it reads as a
    67	paywall before value.
    68	
    69	### 2. Settings → API keys
    70	
    71	`ApiKeys.tsx` already carries Box, Composio, OpenCode and a VPS alias in a consistent
    72	pattern. Flux is one more field of the same shape. This is where a user who skipped
    73	onboarding goes, and where a key gets replaced.
    74	
    75	### 3. The model picker
    76	
    77	Flux tiers first with Auto leading, then the user's own models, then the pinned catalog.
    78	Rows gated per engine on implemented surfaces — an ungated row that 400s is worse than
    79	no row.
    80	
    81	## The superpower moment
    82	
    83	The feeling is not "I configured a router." It is **"I pasted one key and every bot on my
    84	roster just got better."**
    85	
    86	So the paste must be followed IMMEDIATELY by visible proof, not a success toast:
    87	
    88	- Call `GET /v1/models` on paste. Show the real number: **"79 models unlocked."**
    89	- Show the four tiers as one line, already selectable.
    90	- Show which of their existing Embers can now use it.
    91	
    92	That is the Sutherland move: the proof is the product. A spinner and a green tick
    93	delivers the same function and none of the feeling.
    94	
    95	## Copy
    96	
    97	**Onboarding block, under the engine list:**
    98	
    99	> ### One key. Every model. Always the right one.
   100	> Flux Router picks the right model for each job automatically, so you stop paying
   101	> frontier prices for work that didn't need it.
   102	>
   103	> `[ paste your Flux key ]`   **Connect**   ·   *I'll do this later*
   104	>
   105	> Don't have one? **Get a key →**
   106	
   107	**On success (replacing a toast):**
   108	
   109	> **79 models unlocked.**
   110	> Flux Auto · Reasoning · Standard · Fast — plus every pinned model.
   111	> Your Embers can use these now. Nothing else changed.
   112	
   113	That last line matters: it answers "did this break my Claude subscription?" before it
   114	is asked. Krug rule 3.
   115	
   116	**Settings field label:** `Flux Router key` · placeholder `sk-flux-...`
   117	**Helper:** `One key runs every engine. Your existing CLI logins keep working.`
   118	
   119	**Picker row labels:** `Flux Auto — right model, right price` · `Flux Reasoning — thinks
   120	harder` · `Flux Fast — answers quickest` · `Flux Standard — the everyday one`
   121	
   122	## Security constraint, non-negotiable
   123	
   124	**The key is never typed into a chat turn.** Transcripts persist and replay into context,
   125	so a key pasted into a conversation is a key written to disk in cleartext and re-sent to a
   126	model on every future turn. Conversation may PROMPT; a native field must CAPTURE.
   127	
   128	Storage: `~/.murage/config.json` at 0600, same as Sendlane. Added to
   129	`WORKSPACE_CREDENTIAL_ENV` so spawned agents cannot read it, and injected at the surface
   130	layer via the `MURAGE_LOCAL_*` indirection `codexLocalProviderArgs` already uses — which
   131	resolves the contradiction between "no agent sees the key" and "codex needs it at request
   132	time" (Kimi finding C, `flux-router-integration.md`).
   133	
   134	## Prerequisite that must land first
   135	
   136	`OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `OPENAI_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` are
   137	never stripped, and every spawn path spreads `...process.env` (`acp/core.ts:194`,
   138	`codex.ts:102`, `claude.ts:80`). A user running cc-switch silently defeats or corrupts the
   139	Flux surface. Strip first, route second.
   140	
   141	## Scope call for tonight
   142	
   143	- **Ships:** key field, onboarding block, the proof moment, settings entry, picker rows
   144	  gated to implemented surfaces.
   145	- **Does not ship:** the images surface (backend is down), Flux Voice, spend display.

    27	const SECTIONS: Array<{
    28	  id: AppSettingsSection;

    31	  keywords: string[];
    32	}> = [
    33	  { id: "general", label: "General", icon: User, keywords: ["profile", "name", "email", "skin", "theme", "appearance", "analytics", "updates", "tools", "tool calls"] },
    34	  { id: "experimental", label: "Experimental", icon: FlaskConical, keywords: ["early", "preview", "teach", "skill", "browser", "profiles"] },

    37	  { id: "companion", label: "Phone", icon: Smartphone, keywords: ["companion", "phone", "pair", "mobile"] },
    38	  { id: "computer", label: "Local VM", icon: Monitor, keywords: ["vm", "virtual", "desktop"] },
    39	  { id: "usage", label: "Usage", icon: Coins, keywords: ["tokens", "cost", "billing"] },
    40	];
    41	
    42	function sectionMatches(section: (typeof SECTIONS)[number], query: string): boolean {
    43	  if (!query) return true;
    44	  return [section.label, ...section.keywords].some((part) => part.toLowerCase().includes(query));
    45	}

    47	/** Name + email, persisted to /api/config {profile} on blur. */
    48	function ProfileFields() {
    49	  const { state, dispatch } = useStore();
    50	  const [name, setName] = useState(state.config?.profile?.name ?? "");
    51	  const [email, setEmail] = useState(state.config?.profile?.email ?? "");
    52	  useEffect(() => {
    53	    setName(state.config?.profile?.name ?? "");
    54	    setEmail(state.config?.profile?.email ?? "");
    55	  }, [state.config?.profile?.name, state.config?.profile?.email]);
    56	
    57	  const save = () => {
    58	    void fetch("/api/config", {
    59	      method: "PUT",
    60	      headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
    61	      body: JSON.stringify({ profile: { name: name.trim(), email: email.trim().toLowerCase() } }),
    62	    })
    63	      .then((r) => r.json())
    64	      .then((config) => dispatch({ type: "configStatus", config }))
    65	      .catch(() => {});
    66	  };
    67	
    68	  const inputClass =
    69	    "w-full rounded-lg border border-hairline/40 bg-inset px-3 py-2 text-[14px] text-ink placeholder:text-ink-secondary focus:border-hairline focus:outline-none";
    70	  return (
    71	    <div className="flex flex-col gap-3">
    72	      <input value={name} onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)} onBlur={save} placeholder="Your name" className={inputClass} />
    73	      <input
    74	        type="email"
    75	        value={email}
    76	        onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)}
    77	        onBlur={save}
    78	        placeholder="you@example.com"
    79	        className={inputClass}
    80	      />

    21	### 1.1 CONFIRMED WORKING
    22	
    23	| Surface | Endpoint | Verdict |
    24	|---|---|---|
    25	| OpenAI chat-completions | `POST /v1/chat/completions` | **WORKS** — 200, standard `chat.completion` body |
    26	| Anthropic Messages | `POST /anthropic/v1/messages` | **WORKS** — 200, genuine `msg_…` object, SSE streaming confirmed |
    27	| OpenAI Responses | `POST /v1/responses` | **WORKS** — 200, standard `resp_…` object, SSE + function calling confirmed |
    28	| Model discovery | `GET /v1/models` | **WORKS** — 200, lists `flux-auto`, `flux-fast`, `flux-standard`, `flux-reasoning`, `flux-voice`, `flux-image`, `flux-pinned-*` |
    29	
    30	### 1.2 FAILED — DO NOT BUILD AGAINST
    31	
    32	> ### 🔴 `POST /v1/images/generations` IS BROKEN. 8/8 attempts failed.
    33	>
    34	> Every request carrying a real prompt returned a **Cloudflare 502 with a
    35	> 16-byte `text/plain` body — `error code: 502`** — in 0.54–1.68 s, across
    36	> ~10 minutes, including with `-m 300`. Variants tried: with/without `size`,
    37	> with/without `model`, `b64_json`, bad size, distinct prompts. All 502.
    38	>
    39	> The route *exists* and validates (`{}` → 400 `prompt required`; no auth →
    40	> 401 `unauthorized`; `GET` → 405), and `/v1/chat/completions` on the **same
    41	> key** returns 200. So this is an **origin/backend outage on the image
    42	> route**, not our request shape and not a bad key.
    43	>
    44	> **Consequence: the SUCCESS response shape of the image route was never
    45	> observed.** `b64_json` vs `url`, the returned MIME type, and whether
    46	> `quality`/`output_format` are honoured are all unknown. See §7.
    47	
    48	> ### 🔴 THE PROBE ACCOUNT IS 402'd. All inference is currently dead.
    49	>
    50	> Partway through probing — after ~13 billed calls totalling ≈ $0.003 —
    51	> **every** `POST` to `/v1/responses` **and** `/v1/chat/completions` began
    52	> returning `402`:
    53	> `"A prior charge on this account could not be reconciled automatically and
    54	> requires support intervention. Please contact support to restore access.
    55	> Top up at https://fluxrouter.ai/home/billing"`
    56	> Still 402 after a 45 s wait.
    57	>
    58	> **`GET /v1/models` still returns 200 while 402'd.** A models-list health
    59	> check will show green while every turn fails. Do not use `/v1/models` as
    60	> the liveness probe (§6.4).
    61	
    62	### 1.3 Non-blocking behaviours that change the design
    63	
    64	- **`flux-auto` is an alias, not a model.** Observed resolutions across calls:
    65	  `qwen-plus`, `MiniMax-M3`, `deepseek-v4-pro`, `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`.
    66	  Selection is per-request (bandit selector; `x-flux-phase-latency.bandit_ms`).
    67	- **`x-flux-model-window` varied 129024 ↔ 1000000** between calls on the same
    68	  alias. Anything sized against the pinned window overflows when `flux-auto`
    69	  downgrades.
    70	- **The proxy mutates the prompt.** `x-flux-engines-applied:
    71	  r10_11_rtk_compactor,r10_2_semantic_cache,r10_4a_directives` on every call.
    72	  Identical one-word prompts reported 164 `input_tokens` (128 cache_read) on
    73	  one call and 9 on another. **`usage.*_tokens` is unusable for our cost or
    74	  context accounting.** Use the `x-flux-cost-usd` header.
    75	- **A tool named `shell` is silently renamed to `local_shell`** in the
    76	  returned `function_call`. Reproduced 3/3 on `flux-auto` and on two pinned
    77	  models, so it is gateway-level. `get_weather_v2` passed through unchanged.
    78	  This is a codex-CLI hazard (§4.2).
    79	- **`previous_response_id` is rejected by design** — `"flux-router is
    80	  stateless; resend full input without previous_response_id"`.
    81	- **Error codes lie.** On `/v1/responses` an unknown model id (`gpt-4o`,
    82	  `flux-nope`) returns **401 `authentication_error`**, indistinguishable from
    83	  a bad key. On `/anthropic/v1/messages` an unknown model returns **403
    84	  `permission_error`** with the same wording as a genuinely gated model. There
    85	  is no 404 for a bad model id on either surface.
    86	- **Response headers leak tenant internals** — `x-litellm-key-spend`,
    87	  `x-litellm-key-max-budget`, the upstream provider base URL, and on the
    88	  Anthropic route `llm_provider-anthropic-organization-id` /
    89	  `-workspace-id`. **Never log a full header dump.** Whitelist headers (§6.3).
    90	
   369	### 4.4 Engines with no Flux surface
   370	
   371	`opencode`, `qoder` (config-file only), `droid`, `auggie`, `copilot`, `kiro`,
   372	`vibe` (vendor-locked). No injection, no picker rows (§5).
   373	
   374	---
   375	
   376	## 5. Per-engine picker gate
   377	
   378	### 5.1 One table, two consumers
   379	
   380	Export from `server/flux-routing.ts` — the same module that injects — so the
   381	picker and the router can never disagree about which engines are routable:
   382	
   383	```ts
   384	export const FLUX_SURFACE: Partial<Record<DriverKind, "anthropic" | "openai" | "responses">> = {
   385	  claudeAgent: "anthropic",
   386	  qwenAgent:   "openai",
   387	  gooseAgent:  "openai",
   388	  codex:       "responses",
   389	};
   390	```
   391	
   392	Absent key ⇒ not routable ⇒ no rows, and the spawn gate throws (§5.4).
   393	
   394	### 5.2 The gate must NOT live in `mergeLocalInject`
   395	
   396	`mergeLocalInject` (**local-inject.ts:356**) has signature
   397	`(catalog, env, fetchImpl)` — **no engine identity at all** — and is called by
   398	10 drivers (codex-catalog.ts:365 and :431, claude.ts:536, antigravity.ts:398,
   399	pi.ts:180, kimi.ts:505, qwen.ts:84, hermes.ts:367, grok.ts:188, droid.ts:171,
   400	opencode-go.ts:381). Appending Flux rows to its loop (local-inject.ts:373-388)
   401	is the shortest path and is precisely the bug: it fans `flux-*` onto every
   402	engine, routable or not. It is engine-blind by design because local hosts are
   403	engine-agnostic; **Flux is not**.
   404	
   405	### 5.3 Where the gate goes
   406	
   407	New sibling helper with engine identity as a **required** parameter:
   408	
   409	```ts
   410	export function mergeFluxCatalog(
   411	  catalog: ModelCatalog,
   412	  driverKind: DriverKind,
   413	  env: Record<string, string | undefined>,
   414	): ModelCatalog   // returns catalog untouched when FLUX_SURFACE[driverKind] is absent
   415	```
   416	
   417	Called from each driver's own `resolveModels`/`refreshModels`, where
   418	`driverKind` is a compile-time constant. A required parameter makes the gate
   419	unskippable at the type level. For codex the call site is **between
   420	codex-catalog.ts:420 and :431** — after the extras loop closes, before the
   421	`mergeLocalInject` return — and it must emit `flux::flux-auto` style ids
   422	(§4.2), never bare slugs.
   423	
   424	### 5.4 Backstop and spawn-side enforcement
   425	
   426	- **Registry backstop — `server/harness/registry.ts:168`.** `describe()` passes
   427	  `models: inst.models` straight through with no filtering; it is the one choke
   428	  point every catalog crosses to the UI (and it calls `refreshModels()` at :158
   429	  immediately before). Re-filter `flux-`-prefixed rows here against the same
   430	  `FLUX_SURFACE` table, so a driver that forgets the gate still cannot leak.
   431	- **Spawn-side 409 — `server/index.ts`, immediately after the effort re-check
   432	  at :2496.** A client filter cannot be the gate: `checkedModelSelection`
   433	  (**index.ts:729**) only validates ids against the catalog when
   434	  `requireAvailableModel` is true, deliberately (comment at :725-728), so a
   435	  persisted, cloned (store.tsx:1623), imported, or MCP-set `flux-auto`
   436	  selection passes untouched. Throw the same-shaped 409 as the effort check:
   437	  > `this bot's engine cannot route Flux Router — choose another model in settings`
   438	- **Capability flag — `server/contracts.ts:215` block.** Add
   439	  `fluxRouting?: boolean` beside `computerMcp`/`effortLevels`/`queueing`, which
   440	  all carry the doctrine "never offer a control the driver cannot honour". It
   441	  projects through **registry.ts:169-180** into `InstanceInfo.capabilities`
   442	  (store.tsx:383-396) and is what disables the UI toggle with a reason.
   443	
   444	### 5.5 Client side
   445	
   446	The client does **not** filter. `ModelPicker.tsx:192-193` partitions solely on
   447	`option.custom`; the rail splits solely on `access` (engine-rail.ts:6-8);
   448	`ModelCatalog`'s option shape (**contracts.ts:316**) has no field that could
   449	express engine affinity — `provider` is display-only (ModelPicker.tsx:54-61).
   450	The client's only legitimate Flux job is rendering the disabled-toggle reason
   451	from `capabilities.fluxRouting`, never inspecting model ids.
   452	
   453	### 5.6 Ordering makes the gate urgent, not optional
   454	
   455	The plan requires "Flux tiers first (Auto leads)". Flux rows are non-custom, so
   456	they land in `ModelPicker`'s `official` list (:192), and `suggestedModels` fills
   457	the compact 5-row list from catalog order (**custom-models.ts:48**). Ungated,
   458	`flux-auto` becomes the **top suggested row on every engine**, including the
   459	ones that would 400. The gate must land in the same change as the rows.
   460	
   461	---
   462	
   463	## 6. Client behaviour required by the probes
   464	
   465	### 6.1 Never trust the body's `model`

codex
Verdict: **FAIL. Do not build this plan as written for tonight’s demo.** It can report success for a dead account, overstates engine coverage, and never defines whether connecting Flux merely adds choices or silently changes bots.

## 1. Where is the plan wrong?

1. **CRITICAL — the success check is a known false positive.** The plan turns `GET /v1/models` into “79 models unlocked” ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:88](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:88)). The spec records `models=200` while every inference call returned 402, twice, after only 13–15 calls ([flux-router-spec.md:619](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:619), [flux-router-spec.md:628](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:628), [flux-router-spec.md:637](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:637)). This design congratulates the user immediately before their first turn fails.

2. **CRITICAL — “every bot” and “every engine” are false.** The copy claims every bot improves and one key runs every engine ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:83](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:83), [flux-onboarding-ux.md:117](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:117)). The spec supports only Claude, Qwen, Goose, and Codex ([flux-router-spec.md:383](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:383)); seven named engines have no Flux surface ([flux-router-spec.md:369](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:369)).

3. **CRITICAL — “79 models unlocked” is not merely unverified; it is false.** `/v1/models` includes `flux-image` and `flux-voice` ([flux-router-spec.md:23](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:23)). Images are broken, Voice was never probed, and both are explicitly excluded from tonight’s scope ([flux-router-spec.md:32](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:32), [flux-router-spec.md:589](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:589), [flux-onboarding-ux.md:145](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:145)). “Listed” is not “unlocked.”

4. **HIGH — connecting and activating are conflated.** The success state implies an immediate improvement ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:109](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:109)), but the current picker changes a bot only after an explicit model selection ([ModelPicker.tsx:177](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/src/components/ModelPicker.tsx:177)). If Connect silently selects Auto, it violates the non-default requirement. If it does not, the promised transformation did not happen.

5. **HIGH — Flux does not “earn” first place.** The plan hardcodes Flux first with Auto leading ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:75](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:75)). Current picker logic fills its five visible suggestions from catalog order ([custom-models.ts:30](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/src/lib/custom-models.ts:30)); the spec explicitly says this makes Flux Auto the top suggestion ([flux-router-spec.md:453](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:453)). That is self-preferencing, not earned selection.

6. **HIGH — the savings claim has no economic basis in the UI.** “Stop paying frontier prices” ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:99](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:99)) does not reduce an existing subscription merely by connecting another paid service. The plan itself says cost must be answered inline ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:52](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:52)), then omits pricing and spend display ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:145](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:145)).

7. **HIGH — “Nothing else changed” is untrue.** Flux mutates prompts, changes context windows between requests, rejects response continuation, and rewrites Codex’s `shell` tool contract ([flux-router-spec.md:64](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:64), [flux-router-spec.md:70](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:70), [flux-router-spec.md:654](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:654)). That may be acceptable, but it is not “nothing.”

8. **HIGH — secret storage is specified incorrectly.** The plan says plaintext `~/.murage/config.json` at 0600 ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:128](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:128)). Packaged Murage deliberately migrates provider secrets out of that file into Electron’s OS-encrypted store ([workspace-credentials.mjs:1](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/electron/workspace-credentials.mjs:1), [main.mjs:262](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/electron/main.mjs:262)). Flux must join that path.

9. **MEDIUM — the stated recovery location does not exist.** There is no `Settings → API keys`; the real section is `Settings → Connections` ([SettingsModal.tsx:27](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/src/components/SettingsModal.tsx:27)).

## 2. What will users ask that the screen does not answer?

The copy leaves these stalls:

- What is Flux, and is it part of Murage or another company/account?
- How much does it cost? Is there a free credit, minimum top-up, or card requirement?
- Does this add usage charges on top of my Claude/ChatGPT subscriptions?
- Does connecting switch my existing bots, or only add models to their picker?
- Which of my bots actually support it?
- Which “79 models” work now, versus merely appearing in the API list?
- What does Auto optimize: price, speed, quality, privacy, or Flux’s margin?
- Why should I trust “always the right one” or “right price”?
- Where do my prompts go, and what are the retention/privacy terms?
- What happens when Flux is down or out of credit?
- Will Murage fall back to my existing CLI login, or will the turn fail?
- How do I remove the key and return to native routing?
- How will I see which upstream model ran and what it cost?

The document says every such question is a defect, then answers almost none of them ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:47](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:47)).

## 3. Is “79 models unlocked” achievable?

**The number is retrievable; the proof moment is not valid.**

`GET /v1/models` can return 79 entries while the account cannot execute a single turn. Therefore it proves only that the catalog endpoint responded—not that the key is funded, inference is alive, or those models are usable.

The spec already gives the correct validation: a cheap authenticated `POST`, such as `flux-fast` with one output token, with explicit 401/402/403/502 handling ([flux-router-spec.md:491](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:491)). Keep the catalog count separate and call it “models listed,” never “unlocked.”

## 4. Single biggest usability failure mode

**The user cannot tell what Connect changes.**

The design has no explicit choice between:

- “Add Flux as an option,” and
- “Switch supported bots to Flux Auto.”

That ambiguity is especially bad for the author’s own margin product. The current onboarding screen already has a primary `Continue` button ([Onboarding.tsx:287](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/src/components/Onboarding.tsx:287)); adding `Connect` and “I’ll do this later” creates three competing exits without clarifying the consequence of any of them.

## 5. Is the copy good?

Mostly AI-flavoured: rhythmic absolutes, “superpower,” “every,” “always,” “right model,” and “right price.” It sounds confident precisely where the evidence is weakest.

Weakest line:

> One key. Every model. Always the right one.

Rewrite:

> **Stop choosing a model for every job.**  
> Flux Auto balances quality and cost on supported bots. Connecting adds it as an option; nothing switches until you choose it.

That is a real Sutherland reframe without making three false absolute claims.

## 6. What did the plan miss entirely?

- **A consent contract:** exactly what Connect changes, with no silent selection.
- **Failure and recovery UX:** bad key, billing block, unsupported model, malformed-key 500, provider 502, native fallback, and retry suppression.
- **A demo kill switch and backend preflight:** the billing defect recurs mid-session and requires native fallback ([flux-router-spec.md:632](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:632)).
- **OS-encrypted credential plumbing:** workspace credential registry, Electron IPC allowlist, renderer types, migration, diagnostics redaction, replace, and revoke.
- **Pricing, billing, privacy, and retention disclosure.**
- **The actual Sutherland “map”:** show the routed model and Flux-billed cost after a turn. The spec exposes both through whitelisted headers ([flux-router-spec.md:482](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:482)). “79” is inventory; a route-and-cost receipt reduces uncertainty.
- **End-to-end acceptance gates per engine.** Qwen and Claude still have unresolved client behavior, while Codex has a confirmed tool-contract substitution ([flux-router-spec.md:589](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:589), [flux-router-spec.md:668](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:668)).
- **A real isolated-fixture verification plan.** Murage requires workflow evidence, not green unit tests ([verification/README.md:47](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/verification/README.md:47)).

This design congratulates the user immediately before their first turn fails.

2. **CRITICAL — “every bot” and “every engine” are false.** The copy claims every bot improves and one key runs every engine ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:83](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:83), [flux-onboarding-ux.md:117](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:117)). The spec supports only Claude, Qwen, Goose, and Codex ([flux-router-spec.md:383](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:383)); seven named engines have no Flux surface ([flux-router-spec.md:369](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:369)).

3. **CRITICAL — “79 models unlocked” is not merely unverified; it is false.** `/v1/models` includes `flux-image` and `flux-voice` ([flux-router-spec.md:23](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:23)). Images are broken, Voice was never probed, and both are explicitly excluded from tonight’s scope ([flux-router-spec.md:32](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:32), [flux-router-spec.md:589](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:589), [flux-onboarding-ux.md:145](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:145)). “Listed” is not “unlocked.”

4. **HIGH — connecting and activating are conflated.** The success state implies an immediate improvement ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:109](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:109)), but the current picker changes a bot only after an explicit model selection ([ModelPicker.tsx:177](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/src/components/ModelPicker.tsx:177)). If Connect silently selects Auto, it violates the non-default requirement. If it does not, the promised transformation did not happen.

5. **HIGH — Flux does not “earn” first place.** The plan hardcodes Flux first with Auto leading ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:75](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:75)). Current picker logic fills its five visible suggestions from catalog order ([custom-models.ts:30](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/src/lib/custom-models.ts:30)); the spec explicitly says this makes Flux Auto the top suggestion ([flux-router-spec.md:453](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:453)). That is self-preferencing, not earned selection.

6. **HIGH — the savings claim has no economic basis in the UI.** “Stop paying frontier prices” ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:99](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:99)) does not reduce an existing subscription merely by connecting another paid service. The plan itself says cost must be answered inline ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:52](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:52)), then omits pricing and spend display ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:145](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:145)).

7. **HIGH — “Nothing else changed” is untrue.** Flux mutates prompts, changes context windows between requests, rejects response continuation, and rewrites Codex’s `shell` tool contract ([flux-router-spec.md:64](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:64), [flux-router-spec.md:70](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:70), [flux-router-spec.md:654](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:654)). That may be acceptable, but it is not “nothing.”

8. **HIGH — secret storage is specified incorrectly.** The plan says plaintext `~/.murage/config.json` at 0600 ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:128](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:128)). Packaged Murage deliberately migrates provider secrets out of that file into Electron’s OS-encrypted store ([workspace-credentials.mjs:1](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/electron/workspace-credentials.mjs:1), [main.mjs:262](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/electron/main.mjs:262)). Flux must join that path.

9. **MEDIUM — the stated recovery location does not exist.** There is no `Settings → API keys`; the real section is `Settings → Connections` ([SettingsModal.tsx:27](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/src/components/SettingsModal.tsx:27)).

## 2. What will users ask that the screen does not answer?

The copy leaves these stalls:

- What is Flux, and is it part of Murage or another company/account?
- How much does it cost? Is there a free credit, minimum top-up, or card requirement?
- Does this add usage charges on top of my Claude/ChatGPT subscriptions?
- Does connecting switch my existing bots, or only add models to their picker?
- Which of my bots actually support it?
- Which “79 models” work now, versus merely appearing in the API list?
- What does Auto optimize: price, speed, quality, privacy, or Flux’s margin?
- Why should I trust “always the right one” or “right price”?
- Where do my prompts go, and what are the retention/privacy terms?
- What happens when Flux is down or out of credit?
- Will Murage fall back to my existing CLI login, or will the turn fail?
- How do I remove the key and return to native routing?
- How will I see which upstream model ran and what it cost?

The document says every such question is a defect, then answers almost none of them ([flux-onboarding-ux.md:47](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-onboarding-ux.md:47)).

## 3. Is “79 models unlocked” achievable?

**The number is retrievable; the proof moment is not valid.**

`GET /v1/models` can return 79 entries while the account cannot execute a single turn. Therefore it proves only that the catalog endpoint responded—not that the key is funded, inference is alive, or those models are usable.

The spec already gives the correct validation: a cheap authenticated `POST`, such as `flux-fast` with one output token, with explicit 401/402/403/502 handling ([flux-router-spec.md:491](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:491)). Keep the catalog count separate and call it “models listed,” never “unlocked.”

## 4. Single biggest usability failure mode

**The user cannot tell what Connect changes.**

The design has no explicit choice between:

- “Add Flux as an option,” and
- “Switch supported bots to Flux Auto.”

That ambiguity is especially bad for the author’s own margin product. The current onboarding screen already has a primary `Continue` button ([Onboarding.tsx:287](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/src/components/Onboarding.tsx:287)); adding `Connect` and “I’ll do this later” creates three competing exits without clarifying the consequence of any of them.

## 5. Is the copy good?

Mostly AI-flavoured: rhythmic absolutes, “superpower,” “every,” “always,” “right model,” and “right price.” It sounds confident precisely where the evidence is weakest.

Weakest line:

> One key. Every model. Always the right one.

Rewrite:

> **Stop choosing a model for every job.**  
> Flux Auto balances quality and cost on supported bots. Connecting adds it as an option; nothing switches until you choose it.

That is a real Sutherland reframe without making three false absolute claims.

## 6. What did the plan miss entirely?

- **A consent contract:** exactly what Connect changes, with no silent selection.
- **Failure and recovery UX:** bad key, billing block, unsupported model, malformed-key 500, provider 502, native fallback, and retry suppression.
- **A demo kill switch and backend preflight:** the billing defect recurs mid-session and requires native fallback ([flux-router-spec.md:632](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:632)).
- **OS-encrypted credential plumbing:** workspace credential registry, Electron IPC allowlist, renderer types, migration, diagnostics redaction, replace, and revoke.
- **Pricing, billing, privacy, and retention disclosure.**
- **The actual Sutherland “map”:** show the routed model and Flux-billed cost after a turn. The spec exposes both through whitelisted headers ([flux-router-spec.md:482](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:482)). “79” is inventory; a route-and-cost receipt reduces uncertainty.
- **End-to-end acceptance gates per engine.** Qwen and Claude still have unresolved client behavior, while Codex has a confirmed tool-contract substitution ([flux-router-spec.md:589](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:589), [flux-router-spec.md:668](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/plans/flux-router-spec.md:668)).
- **A real isolated-fixture verification plan.** Murage requires workflow evidence, not green unit tests ([verification/README.md:47](/Volumes/Mando/WaylandBots/murage-app/docs/verification/README.md:47)).
